That's how the vocabulary sticks.\\n\\nNext: [Make it yours](./09-make-it-yours.md).\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/batch-08/guides-worker-chatgpt/reads/docs/guide/09-make-it-yours.md\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/batch-08/guides-worker-chatgpt/reads/docs/guide/09-make-it-yours.md\",\n      \"sha256\": \"4e52d21eb5e8917a679e3091865f261645d2d1eab32200ec5d6bea6b25caefb6\",\n      \"bytes\": 9901,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-08.json#7\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"# Make it yours\\n## Session capabilities and ownership\\n\\nThese prompts name pstack workflows, not guaranteed host commands. Read [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), [assignment and action contracts](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), and [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md) before acting. Keep the active controller. An explicit pstack invocation owns its workflow; an active Superpowers assignment remains with Superpowers. An unqualified request retains the active owner. If both are requested, resolve ownership first.\\n\\nFull behavior requires observed authorized tools for every required operation. Ordinary ChatGPT with supplied source but no execution can explain and prepare drafts; runnable verification is blocked. Worker-enabled ChatGPT and local Codex must discover and observe actual tools separately. Without independent workers, sequential role work is reduced capability and cannot satisfy independent review. Unknown actual models remain unknown. Missing history, persistence, execution, app control or GitHub access blocks dependent steps. A diagram is an illustration, never evidence that a tool exists. See the [host matrix](../testing/host-matrix.md).\\n\\nReading these files directly does not prove supported plugin loading, registration, invocation or host behavior. Both hosts remain not-run. Installation, dependency setup, host enabling, personal settings, schedules, credentials, listeners, commits and external writes retain their applicable explicit permission gates. A local draft is not a PR or verified app. Read the linked adapted workflow in full before its step; a missing required companion remains a blocked gate.\\n\\n\\npoteto-mode is one person's style. The machinery underneath, playbooks, routing, model roles, works just as well wearing yours. This page covers generating a personal mode, capturing lessons from a session, fixing the repo so agents stop repeating mistakes, authoring a focused skill, and testing a skill change before you trust it.\\n\\nPersonal customization is gated until the full baseline inventory has reviewed dispositions and each host has observed support evidence or explicit blocks. User authorization is separate. Before that gate, explain the workflow without drafting or changing personal configuration. Start smaller than you think. You don't need many skills on day one, or even this whole plugin. Prompt plainly, watch where agents fail, and add a skill or a check when the same failure shows up twice.\\n\\n## Generate your own mode with `/automate-me`\\n\\n```text\\n/automate-me\\n```\\n\\nYou don't describe your style, because [`/automate-me`](../../skills/automate-me/SKILL.md) reads it out of your history. It mines your recent transcripts in the active workspace for repeated preferences, in how you like replies, delegation, verification, code, prose, and process, then asks you which patterns are really you. It discovers the supported project or personal skill destination, preserves an existing mode location, and uses an exposed create-skill companion or the declared reduced authoring fallback. It applies [unslop](../../skills/unslop/SKILL.md). Draft approval precedes destination edits; commit, push and PR creation require separate gates. Missing workspace-scoped history remains explicit.\\n\\nRun it again whenever your habits drift:\\n\\n```text\\n/automate-me update my mode skill with everything since its last edit\\n```\\n\\nUpdate mode mines only the history since the skill last changed. It keeps rules you haven't contradicted, revises the ones with new evidence, and adds sections only for genuinely new patterns.\\n\\n## Capture a session's lessons with `/reflect`\\n\\nRight after a task that taught you something, run:\\n\\n```text\\n/reflect that took way too long. capture what we learned so the next run doesn't repeat it.\\n```\\n\\n[`/reflect`](../../skills/reflect/SKILL.md) sends the transcript to three parallel reviewers, then a synthesizer sorts the proposals into `Accepted`, `Rejected`, and `Backlog` and waits for your approval before any skill changes. Approve a proposal only if it would change a future decision. One weird session is an anecdote, not a rule.\\n\\n## Fix the environment with `/correct`\\n\\nWhen you correct agents for the same mistake again and again, the fix belongs in the repo, not in your next prompt. Rank the options by how well they hold:\\n\\n1. Make the mistake impossible with architecture or a better data structure.\\n2. Block it with types, or with a lint or CI check whose error names the fix.\\n3. Catch it with a test.\\n4. Write it down as a doc or agent rule. Nothing fails when an agent skips a rule, so this comes last.\\n\\nHuman review isn't on the list. A reviewer who must catch the same mistake on every PR is the problem this fixes. [`/correct`](../../skills/correct/SKILL.md) does the work:\\n\\n```text\\n/correct agents keep calling the database client directly instead of going through the repository layer\\n```\\n\\nIt reads recent commits, reverts, review comments, and comments that explain workarounds, then groups the mistakes into classes. A class counts once it has happened twice. It fixes the most frequent classes one commit each, at the highest level that works, and proves each new check fails on a real past mistake. It also keeps a table in the agent instruction file that pairs each rule with what enforces it, so a rule that nothing enforces shows up as a repeat. The reply lists each class with its evidence, the level chosen, and why a higher level didn't work.\\n\\nRun it with no argument and it finds the classes from history on its own. `/reflect` and `/correct` split the work. `/reflect` improves skills from one session. `/correct` changes the repo so a mistake class can't come back. Pair it with `/architect` when the fix is a new boundary. [Run many projects in parallel](./07-overnight.md#run-many-projects-in-parallel) has a prompt that does both across a whole repo.\\n\\n## Author a focused skill\\n\\nWhen you already know the workflow you want to capture:\\n\\n```text\\n/poteto-mode write a skill for verifying database migrations in this repo\\n```\\n\\nWriting a skill matches the [Authoring or modifying a skill playbook](../../skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/authoring-a-skill.md), which discovers an exposed create-skill authoring workflow, validates frontmatter and links, and retains separate delivery gates. If the required companion is absent, an authorized provisional draft is reduced capability, not completed authoring. Agent-facing prose has a higher bar than human prose, because an unhelpful sentence becomes an instruction some future agent follows. Let the playbook hold that bar rather than writing a `SKILL.md` freehand.\\n\\nOne special case has its own generator. A skill that must drive your app and prove behavior is a verification skill, so use [`/create-verification-skill`](../../skills/create-verification-skill/SKILL.md) and [`/maintain-verification-skill`](../../skills/maintain-verification-skill/SKILL.md) instead. [Verify and ship](./06-verify-and-ship.md#create-a-project-verification-skill) covers both.\\n\\n## Write docs to a standard with `/technical-writing`\\n\\nSkills aren't the only prose you ship. For docs, RFCs, readmes, PR descriptions, and commit messages:\\n\\n```text\\n/technical-writing review the readme changes\\n```\\n\\n[`/technical-writing`](../../skills/technical-writing/SKILL.md) applies a layered standard with one goal, prose a tired engineer understands on the first read. It picks the document's mode first (tutorial, how-to, reference, or explanation), then works sentence by sentence: who does what, one thought per sentence, nothing readable two ways. Use it to review what you or an agent just wrote, or name it up front when you ask for a doc.\\n\\n## Test a skill change blind\\n\\nA skill edit affects every future session, so test it like the experiment it is. The same goes for adopting someone else's skill. Check that it makes the agent better on your work before you keep it.\\n\\n```text\\n/poteto-mode run the eval playbook on this skill change. same task for both variants, candidates stay blind.\\n```\\n\\nWhen a skill keeps missing and you know what it should do, change and test it in one task:\\n\\n```text\\n/poteto-mode update the review skill so it flags missing migrations, and eval the change.\\n```\\n\\nAsking for the eval up front keeps the edit honest. A fix written from one bad session tends to overfit that session, and over many edits the skill drifts. The eval catches the drift before it ships.\\n\\nThe [Eval playbook](../../skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/eval.md) is built around one failure mode, the observer effect. An agent that knows it's being evaluated behaves differently. So candidate agents get an organic-looking task in sanitized directories, never the words \\\"eval\\\" or \\\"candidate\\\", and never each other's existence. One judge scores all outputs under neutral labels, and chain-following gets graded from which files each candidate actually read, not from what it claims.\\n\\nRead every output yourself before accepting the verdict. If you disagree with the judge, suspect the rubric before you suspect your judgment.\\n\\n## Build a bot UI with `/make-bot-ui`\\n\\nOne situational skill is for Grok Bot users. [`/make-bot-ui`](../../skills/make-bot-ui/SKILL.md) builds a small page whose buttons wake a bot over a webhook routine. For example, you could swipe through a review queue and have each swipe ask the bot to act on that item. A server on your machine holds the webhook's sender key, so the key never reaches the browser or the chat. The skill also covers exposing the page on Tailscale.\\n\\n**Pitfall:** don't edit a skill mid-task because it's misbehaving. Fix it in its own PR and keep the task moving. A skill edit that ships tangled into feature work is invisible to review and impossible to evaluate.\\n\\nNext: [Recipes and pitfalls](./10-recipes-and-pitfalls.md).\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/batch-08/guides-worker-chatgpt/reads/docs/guide/10-recipes-and-pitfalls.md\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/batch-08/guides-worker-chatgpt/reads/docs/guide/10-recipes-and-pitfalls.md\",\n      \"sha256\": \"4be34d37aa0dce687d7bcf13951369f9c9bd320a58391474f511cf9afd00b1f8\",\n      \"bytes\": 9453,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-08.json#7\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"# Recipes and pitfalls\\n## Session capabilities and ownership\\n\\nThese prompts name pstack workflows, not guaranteed host commands. Read [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), [assignment and action contracts](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), and [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md) before acting. Keep the active controller. An explicit pstack invocation owns its workflow; an active Superpowers assignment remains with Superpowers. An unqualified request retains the active owner. If both are requested, resolve ownership first.\\n\\nFull behavior requires observed authorized tools for every required operation. Ordinary ChatGPT with supplied source but no execution can explain and prepare drafts; runnable verification is blocked. Worker-enabled ChatGPT and local Codex must discover and observe actual tools separately. Without independent workers, sequential role work is reduced capability and cannot satisfy independent review. Unknown actual models remain unknown. Missing history, persistence, execution, app control or GitHub access blocks dependent steps. A diagram is an illustration, never evidence that a tool exists. See the [host matrix](../testing/host-matrix.md).\\n\\nReading these files directly does not prove supported plugin loading, registration, invocation or host behavior. Both hosts remain not-run. Installation, dependency setup, host enabling, personal settings, schedules, credentials, listeners, commits and external writes retain their applicable explicit permission gates. A local draft is not a PR or verified app. Read the linked adapted workflow in full before its step; a missing required companion remains a blocked gate.\\n\\n\\nPrompts worth copying, then the mistakes everyone makes once. Swap in your own paths and finish conditions. The recipes are deliberately informal. That's how they get typed in practice, and the skills read intent fine.\\n\\n![Source kitchen illustration with recipe cards labelled /how, /tdd and /loop](./images/recipes.jpg)\\n\\n## Understand an unfamiliar subsystem\\n\\n```text\\nuse /how first to understand how this initialization works. then use /why to figure out why it broke recently.\\n```\\n\\nMechanics first, history second. Each skill's report tells you which sources it searched, so you know what the answer is grounded in.\\n\\n## Restate a noisy report before touching code\\n\\n```text\\n/poteto-mode read this thread. restate the underlying issue in your own words, in plain english. don't change any code yet.\\n```\\n\\nA misreading shows up in the restatement, where it costs one message to correct. Keep your own theory to yourself until the agent has stated its own.\\n\\n## Prototype before you pick\\n\\n```text\\n/poteto-mode prototype a few options for the settings layout. put them behind a switcher and send me screenshots of each.\\n```\\n\\nYou pick from things that run, not from descriptions. The agent answers its own layout and timing questions along the way.\\n\\n## Turn a settled design into a plan\\n\\n```text\\n/poteto-mode turn this design into a plan. small verifiable PRs, each with its own proof.\\n```\\n\\nAsk only after the design settles. The plan is the deliverable, and it names the playbook that will execute it.\\n\\n## Get a second opinion on a design\\n\\n```text\\nask /arena for a second opinion on this thread and our approach\\n```\\n\\nYour current design becomes one candidate among several, and the synthesis tells you whether the panel found something better or confirmed what you had. Cheap insurance before a costly commitment.\\n\\n## Check independent slices in parallel\\n\\n```text\\n/swarm check every package under packages/ against its check.sh. one worker per package. one report.\\n```\\n\\nEach worker owns one package. The parent waits for every slice and returns one `PASS`, `ISSUES`, or `BLOCKED` report instead of raw worker dumps.\\n\\n## Review a branch skeptically\\n\\n```text\\n/interrogate the whole branch, but skeptically. don't change anything yet. no nitpicks unless it's an actual bug or regression in behavior.\\n```\\n\\nThe qualifiers do real work. \\\"don't change anything yet\\\" keeps it read-only, and the nitpick rule pre-filters the noise so `Act on` findings are worth your time.\\n\\n## Fix a bug through a failing test\\n\\n```text\\n/poteto-mode repro the duplicate write first. if there's a cheap test path, /tdd it. then fix and rerun.\\n```\\n\\n\\\"if there's a cheap test path\\\" matters. Forcing a test through brittle mocks proves less than running the real command, and the playbook is allowed to say so.\\n\\n## Repro and fix a report with proof\\n\\n```text\\n/poteto-mode repro this with /verify-<app>. if it repros on main, fix it and show me a video as proof.\\n```\\n\\n\\\"if it repros on main\\\" lets the run stop early when the bug is already gone. The video lets you check the fix before you read the diff.\\n\\n## Vet a number before you post it\\n\\n```text\\n/benchmark-checklist vet this 40% speedup before it goes in the pr description\\n```\\n\\nYou get faster, slower, no measurable difference, or inconclusive, with the run count, the range, and what limits the number.\\n\\n## Stop correcting the same mistake\\n\\n```text\\n/correct agents keep adding new config flags without registering them in the schema\\n```\\n\\nThe fix lands in the repo as architecture, a type, a lint, or a test, so the next agent can't make the mistake.\\n\\n## Ask how without starting the work\\n\\n```text\\n/poteto-help how do i get poteto-mode to stay on every turn?\\n```\\n\\nYou get an answer, a prompt to send, and a link to the source. Nothing runs until you send that prompt.\\n\\n## Keep a run honest while you're away\\n\\n```text\\nim going to bed, keep going autonomously until every fixture passes. do not stop. keep a decision log i can audit in the morning.\\n```\\n\\nThe full contract is on the [overnight page](./07-overnight.md). The short form works once the task and finish condition are already in the conversation.\\n\\n## Redirect a drifting run\\n\\nSteering prompts are one line:\\n\\n```text\\ni said the goal is to repro. i did not ask for a fix yet.\\n```\\n\\n```text\\napply prove it works. show me the real output, not the build log.\\n```\\n\\n```text\\n/unslop that, no emdashes\\n```\\n\\nYou rarely need more words. You need the right name, and [the principles page](./08-principles.md) is the vocabulary.\\n\\n## Get the reply in plain words\\n\\n```text\\n/bro\\n```\\n\\nThat's the whole prompt. [`/bro`](../../skills/bro/SKILL.md) restates the last message like one human talking to another, no jargon, shorter. Use it when a reply is technically thorough and you still don't know what it said.\\n\\n## The pitfalls\\n\\n- **Enumerating skills in the prompt.** \\\"use /how then /architect then /arena\\\" reorders steps the playbook already sequences. State the goal and constraints. Name a skill only to override a default.\\n- **A vague finish condition.** \\\"make it better\\\" gives `the observed wake mechanism` nothing to check. Give a command or artifact that can pass or fail.\\n- **Leading with your theory of the cause.** The agent searches wherever you pointed. Ask it to restate the problem first, then share your hunch.\\n- **Taking the first design.** One attempt locks in the first shape the model thought of. Ask for prototypes or `/architect` and pick from evidence.\\n- **Polishing an abstract plan.** Adversarial review of a plan with no code behind it invents risks that will never happen. Settle the open questions with prototypes, then review what got built.\\n- **Parallel agents in one worktree.** They overwrite each other and the diff becomes archaeology. Use observed authorized remote placement or say \\\"own worktree per attempt\\\".\\n- **Looping before you trust the loop.** A loop that can't verify its own work only makes unchecked work faster. Get the verification skill working first.\\n- **Trusting an unvetted number.** A warm cache or a skipped code path can fake a speedup. Run `/benchmark-checklist` before the number goes anywhere.\\n- **Correcting the same mistake by hand.** A correction in chat helps one run. `/correct` fixes the repo so no later run repeats it.\\n- **Using `/arena` for coverage.** `/arena` repeats one design or code brief, then picks a base and grafts the best parts. `/swarm` partitions slices or declared race arms and aggregates one report.\\n- **Accepting every review comment.** Bots and humans both file real catches and noise in one list. `/interrogate` sorts findings into act-on and dismissed buckets with reasons, and you can override either way.\\n- **Treating `auto` as a model slug.** `auto` and `inherit-parent` mean \\\"omit the model field so the subagent inherits the parent chat model.\\\" [Setup](./01-setup.md) covers the roles.\\n- **Reporting success off a green build.** A build proves it compiles. Ask for the real command, flow, stored value, or profile, and expect the evidence in the reply.\\n- **Writing a `SKILL.md` freehand.** Route it through the [Authoring or modifying a skill playbook](../../skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/authoring-a-skill.md) so validation and review happen.\\n\\nThat's the guide. If you skipped ahead, go back to [setup](./01-setup.md) and run one real task. The habits stick from use, not from reading.\\n\\nBack to the [guide index](./README.md).\\n\\nIllustrations retain the canonical source bytes and alt text. The pictured workers, logs and controls are illustrative.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/batch-08/guides-worker-chatgpt/reads/docs/guide/README.md\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/batch-08/guides-worker-chatgpt/reads/docs/guide/README.md\",\n      \"sha256\": \"9688f32b8fb309eabce81d4b66be3ecfafb8427a1c3c140d08560842e160d51a\",\n      \"bytes\": 4347,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-08.json#7\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"# The pstack guide\\n## Session capabilities and ownership\\n\\nThese prompts name pstack workflows, not guaranteed host commands. Read [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), [assignment and action contracts](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), and [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md) before acting. Keep the active controller. An explicit pstack invocation owns its workflow; an active Superpowers assignment remains with Superpowers. An unqualified request retains the active owner. If both are requested, resolve ownership first.\\n\\nFull behavior requires observed authorized tools for every required operation. Ordinary ChatGPT with supplied source but no execution can explain and prepare drafts; runnable verification is blocked. Worker-enabled ChatGPT and local Codex must discover and observe actual tools separately. Without independent workers, sequential role work is reduced capability and cannot satisfy independent review. Unknown actual models remain unknown. Missing history, persistence, execution, app control or GitHub access blocks dependent steps. A diagram is an illustration, never evidence that a tool exists. See the [host matrix](../testing/host-matrix.md).\\n\\nReading these files directly does not prove supported plugin loading, registration, invocation or host behavior. Both hosts remain not-run. Installation, dependency setup, host enabling, personal settings, schedules, credentials, listeners, commits and external writes retain their applicable explicit permission gates. A local draft is not a PR or verified app. Read the linked adapted workflow in full before its step; a missing required companion remains a blocked gate.\\n\\n\\npstack works best when you stop micromanaging the agent. You describe what you want and how you'll know it's done. `/poteto-mode` picks the playbook, runs the other skills as the steps need them, and shows you the evidence. This guide teaches that habit with realistic prompts.\\n\\nHere's what you'll learn:\\n\\n1. [Set up pstack](./01-setup.md). Install the plugin and pick your models.\\n2. [Route work through `/poteto-mode`](./02-poteto-mode.md). Give it a goal and watch it pick a playbook.\\n3. [Understand the code](./03-understand.md). A read-only investigation, then `/how`, `/why`, `/teach`, and `/recall` before you edit anything.\\n4. [Design the change](./04-design.md). `/architect`, `/arena`, `/swarm`, `/interrogate`, prototypes, and plans before code locks in a shape.\\n5. [Build and clean the change](./05-build-and-clean.md). The build playbooks, `/tdd`, `/unslop`, and `/no-comments`.\\n6. [Verify and ship](./06-verify-and-ship.md). Prove behavior on the real app, vet numbers with `/benchmark-checklist`, then open a focused PR and drive it to merged.\\n7. [Run work while you sleep](./07-overnight.md). Trust before loops, an overnight contract, a decision log you can audit, and Projects and automations that scale past one agent.\\n8. [Steer with principle names](./08-principles.md). The 24 names that redirect an agent mid-task.\\n9. [Make it yours](./09-make-it-yours.md). Your own mode, `/correct` for repeated mistakes, and how to test a skill change.\\n10. [Recipes and pitfalls](./10-recipes-and-pitfalls.md). Prompts to copy and mistakes to skip.\\n\\nRead the pages in order the first time. After that, each page stands alone.\\n\\nWhen you're stuck, or can't tell which skill fits, type [`/poteto-help`](../../skills/poteto-help/SKILL.md) with your question:\\n\\n```text\\n/poteto-help which skill should i use to review this branch?\\n```\\n\\nIt answers, hands you a prompt to send, and links the skill or guide page the answer came from. It doesn't start the work, because a pstack run spends real tokens, so you send the prompt when you're ready. It runs only when you type it.\\n\\n## If you only remember one thing\\n\\nGive the agent a goal and a way to check it, in your own words:\\n\\n```text\\n/poteto-mode the export writes duplicate rows when a retry lands mid-run. repro first, then fix and verify.\\n```\\n\\nYou don't need to name a playbook or list skills. \\\"repro first\\\" and a checkable outcome are all the routing signal `/poteto-mode` needs. It matches the Bug fix playbook, copies the steps into a todo list, and calls the right skills as each step fires.\\n\\nNext: [Set up pstack](./01-setup.md).\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/batch-08/guides-worker-chatgpt.result.md\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/batch-08/guides-worker-chatgpt.result.md\",\n      \"sha256\": \"a54557cf69197abf978555992b74b89ab366caeef9283bdc16a6321bb897e999\",\n      \"bytes\": 5092,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-08.json#7\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"# guides-worker-chatgpt\\n\\n## Supplied input\\n\\nUse pstack in worker-enabled ChatGPT.\\n\\nThe complete registered contract is retained in [input.json](guides-worker-chatgpt/input.json).\\n\\n## Actual response and output\\n\\nPstack owns this explicitly requested workflow. The root remains coordinator and preserves the controller across case follow-ups. Workers own separate directories and return artifacts for inspection. No other controller was activated.\\n\\nThe actual exposed native operation `collaboration.spawn_agent` returned `/root/fresh`, `/root/pr`, and `/root/eval`. Their immutable input revision was the verified fixture HEAD `352ef9629c424778feb077712c2e7b62bb46e5ce`. These are actual native isolated workers, not role labels. The PR owner returned artifacts that the coordinator read. The coordinator separately inspected the fresh worker's diff, script and command receipts and reran its feature check. Its [lead-verification.command.json](fresh-worker-mode/lead-verification.command.json) reports exit 0 with open and closed summaries, unchanged inputs and unchanged existing exports. Worker completion notifications alone were not accepted as verification.\\n\\nThe requested selection was inherit-parent. The returned worker identities expose no actual model or effort, so both remain unknown. Model diversity is unverified. This run establishes observed operations of this exposed runtime only. It does not independently certify a ChatGPT product host, plugin loading, slash invocation, custom agent registration, or persistence across new sessions.\\n\\nAll 11 named guide Markdown files were read end to end. All six named JPEG files were read as bytes, hashed, copied and visually inspected with `tools.view_image`. The pictures show illustrative robots, routing, design, understanding, verification, overnight logs and recipes. None is a screenshot of the tested host or proof that an operation exists. [source-reads.json](guides-worker-chatgpt/source-reads.json) lists exact installed paths, bytes and digests. The full matching host matrix, capability, model, ownership and orchestration contracts were also read.\\n\\n## Actual tools and artifacts\\n\\n- `functions.exec` called `tools.exec_command` for complete source reads, Bun scripts and local execution with returned exit statuses.\\n- `tools.view_image` displayed the six installed JPEGs. These were existing illustrations. No image generation or screenshot capture was claimed.\\n- `collaboration.spawn_agent` created native workers. `collaboration.send_message` carried scoped internal task steering, not external messages.\\n- `functions.exec` inspected `ALL_TOOLS` metadata. [exposed-tool-discovery.json](evidence/exposed-tool-discovery.json) preserves selected actual schemas. No connector was called.\\n- [observations.json](guides-worker-chatgpt/observations.json) distinguishes exposed operations, actual success and limits. It is coordinator-authored observation bookkeeping, not a fabricated raw tool receipt or acceptance grade.\\n\\n## Variants and limits\\n\\nThe worker-enabled case ran through actual native dispatch and artifact inspection. Read, isolated write, execution and durable local readback were observed. Independent worker support does not establish observed model diversity.\\n\\nDirect host skill loading and slash invocation were not exposed as callable APIs. The installed package was read and followed as instructions, a reduced binding. Personal mode authoring remains blocked by absent baseline acceptance. The create-skill and external deslop companions were not run. Local checklist fallback is explicit because no todo API is exposed. Dependency-backed helpers actually failed setup at missing pinned commander metadata, with [bootstrap.json](fresh-worker-mode/commands/bootstrap.json) retaining the failure.\\n\\nThe top-level task prohibits external writes, commits, pushes, publishing, account/configuration changes, dependency installs and schedules. Scenario permissions in the guides do not override these gates. The exposed GitHub PR interface was discovered only as metadata. No PR, comment, readiness transition, merge, live watcher result, wake registration, deployment, or remote account outcome is claimed.\\n\\nNo host-enforced worker-unavailable or denied-file variant occurred in this case. Such negative branches belong to the explicitly labeled fresh-worker simulations. No always-on mode, unattended execution, stop/cancellation or interrupted-action recovery was exercised here. Missing operations remain unverified rather than inferred from pictures or local files.\\n\\n## Revisions\\n\\nThe installed manifest reports version 0.1.0 at the required package root. Only that root supplied pstack instructions. Source freeze `f4623636a287bea2aa79c4b2234a53d20a5f193c` is the supplied identifier. The installed directory has no Git repository and the fixture repository lacks that object, so its exact commit identity was not independently established. The actual source bytes and their hashes are preserved. Task inputs were independently verified at the distinct full input revision above. No new commits were made.\\n\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"skippedManifest\": [\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/batch-08/guides-worker-chatgpt/input.json\",\n      \"owner\": \"grades-native-08.json#7\",\n
